### 3. user (2026-06-23T12:41:05.836Z)

1	# Move KhelSutra Studio onto an L4 GPU VM (off the droplet)
2	
3	Self-serve runbook to run the live engine (behind `api./app.khelsutra.guru`) on a **GCP L4 GPU VM**
4	instead of the CPU droplet, and **cut the Cloudflare tunnel over** so traffic lands on the VM. Lets
5	you switch detection between **Gemini** (cloud API, proven) and **WASB native** (uses the L4 GPU).
6	
7	This extends [`DEPLOY_ALPHA.md`](DEPLOY_ALPHA.md) (which deployed the same engine on the droplet) — it
8	reuses the droplet config templates in [`deploy/droplet/`](droplet/) and the GPU-VM scripts in the
9	**engine repo** (`badminton-highlight-indexer`: `deploy/gcp/`).
10	
11	> **These are manual steps today.** Automating them with a one-shot **startup-script** you feed into
12	> the GCP console (`create_gpu_vm.sh` already injects a metadata startup-script — today it only installs
13	> the Ops Agent) is backlogged: engine repo `docs/BACKLOG.md` → *Batch / declarative orchestration*. The
14	> design hinge there is keeping the secrets (Gemini key / Access AUD / tunnel token) out of plaintext
15	> metadata.
16	
17	> **Cost ⚠️** A `g2-standard-4` (1×L4) bills **~$0.70–0.85/hr ≈ ~$500–600/month** while it runs. You
18	> keep it up, so this is an ongoing cost. **`bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` is the only way to
19	> stop billing** (a *stopped* VM still bills its disk). For bursty/intermittent load, a scale-to-zero
20	> **Cloud Run GPU *service*** would be far cheaper (~$0 idle) — that's the backlogged "M7" and a bigger
21	> build; this VM is the fast path that works today.
22	>
23	> **Cutover is safe + reversible.** Bring the VM up and **verify it serving** before touching the
24	> tunnel; the droplet stays as a hot fallback. The tunnel is **token-managed** (ingress lives in the
25	> Cloudflare dashboard, not on the box), so moving it is just "run the same connector on the VM, then
26	> stop it on the droplet" — **no DNS change, no new Cloudflare token, no CF-Access change.**
27	
28	---
29	
30	## 0. Prerequisites (gather these once)
31	
32	- **gcloud** authenticated as the project owner (`gcloud auth login`) on the machine you run this from.
33	- **GPU quota** `GPUS_ALL_REGIONS ≥ 1` and the SA Ops-Agent roles — see the engine repo
34	  `deploy/gcp/README.md` §2. (You already have L4 quota=1 from the nightly runs.)
35	- **The two secrets**, which you already have from the droplet deploy:
36	  - the **rotated Gemini API key** (Drive: `gemini.api_key.txt`) — only needed for the Gemini path;
37	  - the **CF-Access AUD** + team domain — already in the droplet's `/srv/khelsutra/config.json`
38	    (`security.cf_access_aud` / `cf_team_domain`); reuse the same values.
39	  - the **tunnel token** — in the droplet's cloudflared unit (`systemctl cat cloudflared` → the
40	    `--token …` value). You'll copy it to the VM in §6.
41	- **For the WASB path only:** the weights at `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`.
42	
43	You'll work in **two repo checkouts** on your machine: this one (`khelsutra`) for the config templates,
44	and the **engine repo** (`badminton-highlight-indexer`) for the GPU-VM scripts. Commands below say which.
45	
46	---
47	
48	## 1. Provision the L4 VM  *(engine repo)*
49	
50	```bash
51	# in the badminton-highlight-indexer checkout
52	RALLY_VM_NAME=khelsutra-gpu RALLY_GPU=l4 bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
53	```
54	
55	It sweeps zones (asia-south1 first — Mumbai, near your users + co-located with the bucket), uses a
56	driver-ready Deep-Learning-VM image (so `nvidia-smi` works immediately), and bakes in the Ops Agent.
57	**Note the zone it prints** (e.g. `asia-south1-b`) — you need it for SSH/teardown.
58	
59	```bash
60	ZONE=<the-zone-it-printed>
61	gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE   # confirm: nvidia-smi shows the L4
62	```
63	
64	---
65	
66	## 2. Put the engine on the VM
67	
68	The engine repo is private and the VM has no git creds, so push the source from your machine
69	(byte-for-byte the same trick the droplet used):
70	
71	```bash
72	# in the badminton-highlight-indexer checkout
73	git archive --format=tar origin/master \
74	  | gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE --command \
75	    'sudo mkdir -p /opt/khelsutra/engine && sudo chown -R $USER /opt/khelsutra && tar -x -C /opt/khelsutra/engine'
76	```
77	
78	Then on the VM (`gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE`):
79	
80	```bash
81	# ffmpeg + cv2 libs. The DLVM image is Ubuntu 22.04 → libglib2.0-0 (on Ubuntu 24.04 it's libglib2.0-0t64).
82	sudo apt-get update -qq && sudo apt-get install -y ffmpeg libgl1 libglib2.0-0
83	sudo mkdir -p /srv/khelsutra/incoming && sudo chown -R "$USER" /srv/khelsutra
84	cd /opt/khelsutra/engine
85	python3 -m venv /opt/khelsutra/venv
86	/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/pip install -q --upgrade pip
87	/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/pip install -q -e '.[auth,storage-gcs]'   # auth = the Cf-Access JWT verify
88	```
89	
90	`backend.main` is torch-free, so this installs fast. (Torch only comes in with the WASB path, §3.)
91	
92	> **⚠ Run everything as ONE user (don't reuse the droplet's `khelsutra` system user here).** On the
93	> droplet the engine ran as a locked-down `User=khelsutra`; on this GPU VM the simplest, mistake-proof
94	> setup is to keep the engine, the venv, **and the WASB conda env (§3) all owned by your VM login user**
95	> (`whoami` after `gcloud compute ssh`, e.g. your Google id). A split owner is the #1 footgun here:
96	> the WASB env lands in `~/miniconda3` (your home), which a different service user can't read, so a
97	> WASB run fails with a cryptic permission/`WASB_PYTHON not found`. §4 sets the systemd `User=` to this
98	> same login user. (If you insist on the hardened `khelsutra` user, you must install the venv **and**
99	> the conda env into a path it can read, e.g. under `/opt`, and `chown` them to `khelsutra`.)
100	
101	---
102	
103	## 3. *(WASB path only)* Build the native GPU detection env
104	
105	Skip this if you'll only run Gemini. For WASB-on-the-L4:
106	
107	```bash
108	# on the VM, in /opt/khelsutra/engine
109	bash deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh        # builds the `wasb` conda env (~/miniconda3/envs/wasb, torch 1.11+cu113, clones WASB-SBDT)
110	mkdir -p ~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights
111	gcloud storage cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar \
112	  ~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar
113	```
114	
115	The native runner needs **three** env vars (set them in `engine.env`, §4): `WASB_PYTHON`,
116	`WASB_REPO_DIR`, `WASB_WEIGHTS`. `WASB_WEIGHTS` alone is **not** enough (validated on an L4, 2026-06-22).
117	
118	---
119	
120	## 4. Serving config + start
121	
122	Reuse the droplet templates from this repo's [`deploy/droplet/`](droplet/) — the **AUD, team domain,
123	and hosts are identical** to the droplet (it's the same tunnel + Access app).
124	
125	**`/srv/khelsutra/config.json`** (from `config.json.example`): keep `security.edge_auth_enabled=true`,
126	`allowed_video_root=/srv/khelsutra/incoming`, your real `cf_team_domain` + `cf_access_aud`. Set
127	`indexing.default_segmenter` per §5.
128	
129	**`/etc/khelsutra/engine.env`** (from `engine.env.example`):
130	```bash
131	GEMINI_API_KEY=<your rotated key>           # for the Gemini path
132	RALLY_APP_HOME=/srv/khelsutra
133	RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1   # REQUIRED (Host-guard) — load-bearing
134	RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1                    # cloudflared dials 127.0.0.1:8000
135	RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS=https://app.khelsutra.guru
136	# --- WASB path only (add these). Use YOUR login user's real home — print it with: echo $HOME ---
137	WASB_PYTHON=$HOME/miniconda3/envs/wasb/bin/python
138	WASB_REPO_DIR=$HOME/models/WASB-SBDT
139	WASB_WEIGHTS=$HOME/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar
140	```
141	> systemd does **not** expand `$HOME` — paste the literal path (`echo $HOME` → e.g. `/home/yourid`).
142	
143	**`/etc/systemd/system/khelsutra-engine.service`** — start from `deploy/droplet/khelsutra-engine.service`,
144	but make **two** edits for this VM:
145	- `User=` → your **VM login user** (`whoami`), NOT `khelsutra` (see the §2 one-user callout);
146	- `WorkingDirectory=/opt/khelsutra/engine` (matches §2).
147	
148	Then:
149	
150	```bash
151	sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl enable --now khelsutra-engine
152	# local verification (still serving via the droplet at this point):
153	curl -s localhost:8000/api/health                 # 200
154	curl -s localhost:8000/api/capabilities | python3 -m json.tool   # check segmenters + gpu.nvidia_smi_present
155	```
156	
157	If it refuses to start, that's the boot-posture guard doing its job — fix the AUD/key/hosts and retry.
158	
159	---
160	
161	## 5. Switch detection: Gemini ↔ WASB
162	
163	The server default is one config flip + restart. (API callers can also override per request by sending
164	`"segmenter": "wasb_hybrid"` in `POST /api/process`.)
165	
166	| | **Gemini** (proven, no GPU) | **WASB native** (uses the L4) |
167	|---|---|---|
168	| `config.json` → `indexing.default_segmenter` | `"gemini"` | `"wasb_hybrid"` |
169	| `config.json` → `indexing` also | — | `"detector_impl": "native"`, `"skip_ai_handoff": true` |
170	| `engine.env` needs | `GEMINI_API_KEY` | the 3 `WASB_*` vars (§3/§4) |
171	| Prereq | a Gemini key | the WASB env + weights (§3) |
172	| GPU used? | no (cloud API) | **yes** |
173	| Per-match cost | Gemini API tokens | none (pure-engine) |
174	
175	Apply: edit `config.json`, then `sudo systemctl restart khelsutra-engine`. Confirm with
176	`curl -s localhost:8000/api/capabilities` (`default_segmenter`) and a real run.
177	
178	> Start on **Gemini** (it's exactly what's live now), prove the VM serves end-to-end, cut the tunnel
179	> over (§6), then flip to **WASB** once you're happy — so you change one variable at a time.
180	
181	---
182	
183	## 6. Cut the tunnel over to the VM
184	
185	The tunnel `khelsutra-studio` is **token-managed**: its ingress (`api.khelsutra.guru` → `localhost:8000`)
186	lives in the Cloudflare dashboard and follows whichever machine runs the connector. So:
187	
188	```bash
189	# 1. get the token from the droplet (it's your secret; don't paste it anywhere public)
190	ssh root@<droplet-ip> 'systemctl cat cloudflared' | grep -o -- '--token [A-Za-z0-9._=-]*'
191	
192	# 2. on the VM: install cloudflared + run the SAME token
193	gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE
194	  curl -fsSL https://pkg.cloudflare.com/cloudflared-stable-linux-amd64.deb -o /tmp/cf.deb && sudo dpkg -i /tmp/cf.deb
195	  sudo cloudflared service install <THE_TOKEN>     # installs + starts the connector as a service
196	  sudo systemctl status cloudflared                # "Registered tunnel connection" → the VM is now a replica
197	```
198	
199	At this point **both** the droplet and the VM serve the tunnel (Cloudflare HA — either can answer; both
200	run the same engine, so that's fine). Verify the VM is healthy through the public hostname:
201	
202	```bash
203	curl -sI https://api.khelsutra.guru/api/health     # 200 (or the 302→Access login if unauthenticated — that's correct)
204	```
205	
206	Then **stop the droplet's connector** so all traffic lands on the VM:
207	
208	```bash
209	ssh root@<droplet-ip> 'systemctl stop cloudflared && systemctl disable cloudflared'
210	```
211	
212	DNS, the CF-Access app, and the dashboard ingress are unchanged — only the connector host moved.
213	
214	---
215	
216	## 7. Verify the live app is on the VM
217	
218	- `https://app.khelsutra.guru` → log in (email OTP) → upload a match → process → pick rallies → **Build**
219	  → download. (Compile is async — it polls; a few minutes is normal.)
220	- The engine's logs are now on the VM: `gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE --command 'journalctl -u khelsutra-engine -n 50 --no-pager'`.
221	- Per-job proof of which detector ran: `GET /api/jobs/<id>` → `result.compute.path` / the segmenter in the message.
222	
223	---
224	
225	## 8. Rollback (if anything's off)
226	
227	The droplet is untouched (engine still installed, just its cloudflared stopped):
228	
229	```bash
230	ssh root@<droplet-ip> 'systemctl enable --now cloudflared'   # droplet serves again
231	gcloud compute ssh khelsutra-gpu --zone=$ZONE --command 'sudo systemctl stop cloudflared'   # VM stops serving
232	```
233	
234	(Run the droplet start **before** stopping the VM for zero-gap rollback.)
235	
236	---
237	
238	## 9. Cost control / teardown
239	
240	- **Stop billing entirely:** `RALLY_GCP_ZONE=$ZONE bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` (engine repo) —
241	  **delete**, never *stop* (a stopped VM still bills its ~$15–35/mo disk).
242	- Leaving it up is ~$500–600/mo. If load is intermittent, consider the scale-to-zero Cloud Run GPU
243	  *service* (backlogged) instead.
244	- Observability: the VM has the Ops Agent — GPU/CPU/mem graphs are in Cloud Monitoring.
245	
246	---
247

253	| WASB conda env builder | engine repo `deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh` |
254	| Serving config templates | this repo `deploy/droplet/{config.json.example,engine.env.example,khelsutra-engine.service}` |
255	| Tunnel ingress (managed in CF dashboard) | Zero Trust → Networks → Tunnels → `khelsutra-studio` |
256	| The original droplet deploy | [`DEPLOY_ALPHA.md`](DEPLOY_ALPHA.md) |

### 10. user (2026-06-23T12:41:49.916Z)

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Create a rally GPU VM that ALWAYS installs the Ops Agent (Observability). This is THE one way to
3	# spin up a rally GPU box — it bakes the ops-agent startup-script into the VM metadata, attaches the
4	# rally-worker service account (+ cloud-platform scope so it can read/write the bucket + report
5	# metrics), and uses a driver-ready Deep-Learning-VM image (nvidia-smi works immediately).
6	#
7	# It sweeps zones for GPU capacity — asia-south1 (Mumbai, co-located with the bucket) first, then
8	# asia-southeast1 (Singapore), which L4 frequently falls back to when Mumbai is stocked-out.
9	#
10	#   bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh                          # L4 (default), name rally-gpu
11	#   RALLY_VM_NAME=rally-m7 RALLY_GPU=t4 bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
12	#
13	# Prereq (once per project): bash deploy/gcp/provision.sh   (enables logging/monitoring + grants the
14	# SA the Ops-Agent writer roles) AND the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota >= 1 (README §2).
15	#
16	# ⚠ DELETE when done (a running GPU VM is the only real cost):
17	#     RALLY_GCP_ZONE=<zone-it-printed> bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete
18	set -uo pipefail
19	
20	PROJECT="${RALLY_GCP_PROJECT:-gen-lang-client-0306026826}"
21	NAME="${RALLY_VM_NAME:-rally-gpu}"
22	SA="rally-worker@${PROJECT}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
23	GPU="${RALLY_GPU:-l4}"     # l4 | t4
24	HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
25	STARTUP="$HERE/ops-agent-startup.sh"
26	export CLOUDSDK_CORE_DISABLE_PROMPTS=1
27	
28	ZONES="${RALLY_GCP_ZONES:-asia-south1-a asia-south1-b asia-south1-c asia-southeast1-a asia-southeast1-b asia-southeast1-c}"
29	
30	if [ "$GPU" = "l4" ]; then
31	  MACHINE="g2-standard-4"; ACCEL=()          # L4 is integral to the g2 machine family
32	else
33	  MACHINE="n1-standard-8"; ACCEL=(--accelerator=type=nvidia-tesla-t4,count=1)
34	fi
35	
36	ERR="$(mktemp)"
37	for Z in $ZONES; do
38	  echo "== trying $NAME ($GPU) in $Z =="
39	  if gcloud compute instances create "$NAME" --project="$PROJECT" --zone="$Z" \
40	       --machine-type="$MACHINE" "${ACCEL[@]}" \
41	       --maintenance-policy=TERMINATE --provisioning-model=STANDARD \
42	       --image-family=common-cu129-ubuntu-2204-nvidia-580 \
43	       --image-project=deeplearning-platform-release \
44	       --boot-disk-size=120GB --boot-disk-type=pd-balanced \
45	       --service-account="$SA" --scopes=cloud-platform \
46	       --metadata-from-file=startup-script="$STARTUP" 2>"$ERR"; then
47	    echo "✅ CREATED $NAME in $Z — the Ops Agent installs on boot (Observability ~2-3 min later)."
48	    echo "   SSH:    gcloud compute ssh $NAME --zone=$Z"
49	    echo "   DELETE: RALLY_GCP_ZONE=$Z bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete"
50	    rm -f "$ERR"; exit 0
51	  fi
52	  if grep -qiE "STOCKOUT|does not have enough" "$ERR"; then
53	    echo "  (no $GPU capacity in $Z — trying the next zone)"; continue
54	  fi
55	  echo "  create failed:"; tail -4 "$ERR"; rm -f "$ERR"; exit 1
56	done
57	echo "❌ No $GPU capacity in any swept zone ($ZONES). Try again later or RALLY_GPU=t4."
58	rm -f "$ERR"; exit 1
59

===ENGINE.ENV.EXAMPLE===
# KhelSutra engine — environment for the alpha droplet (the `api.` side).
# Copy to /etc/khelsutra/engine.env, fill the secret, and `chmod 600` it.
# Loaded by khelsutra-engine.service. Verified against engine origin/master
# (see ../DEPLOY_ALPHA.md §2 + khelsutra PR #78).

# --- Secret (rotate the chat-shared one; Gemini is paid + uploads footage to Google) ---
GEMINI_API_KEY=PASTE_YOUR_ROTATED_KEY_HERE

# --- App home: config.json, .env, the SQLite DB and output/ all resolve UNDER here (engine P0a).
#     The engine reads $RALLY_APP_HOME/config.json — so the security block below lives at
#     /srv/khelsutra/config.json (NOT the repo root). Use `--setup` so writer/reader agree. ---
RALLY_APP_HOME=/srv/khelsutra

# --- Exposure: the two vars the original kit MISSED. Without them the tunnel is dead-on-arrival. ---
# edge_auth_enabled=true flips the bind to 0.0.0.0 AND keeps the Host-header guard loopback-only,
# so it boots "healthy" then 400s every request cloudflared forwards with Host: api.khelsutra.guru.
RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS=api.khelsutra.guru,localhost,127.0.0.1   # REQUIRED — load-bearing fix (allowlist is decoupled from the bind); loopback added for on-box health checks
RALLY_BIND_HOST=127.0.0.1                              # keep the loopback bind (cloudflared dials 127.0.0.1:8000 anyway)
RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS=https://app.khelsutra.guru    # lock CORS to the SPA origin

1	# KhelSutra highlight engine (FastAPI, the `api.` side) — alpha droplet systemd unit.
2	#
3	# Adjust the two paths marked <SET> to your droplet's layout, then:
4	#   sudo cp khelsutra-engine.service /etc/systemd/system/
5	#   sudo systemctl daemon-reload
6	#   sudo systemctl enable --now khelsutra-engine
7	#   journalctl -u khelsutra-engine -f      # watch the boot posture (PR #294) print
8	#
9	# The engine binds 127.0.0.1:8000 (RALLY_BIND_HOST in the env file); cloudflared dials that.
10	# It fails-fast (exit 1, "[STARTUP] refusing to start") if edge_auth is on but the jail/AUD
11	# are missing — so a bad config.json surfaces here immediately.
12	
13	[Unit]
14	Description=KhelSutra highlight engine (FastAPI)
15	After=network-online.target
16	Wants=network-online.target
17	
18	[Service]
19	Type=simple
20	User=khelsutra
21	Group=khelsutra
22	# <SET> the engine repo checkout on the droplet:
23	WorkingDirectory=/opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer
24	EnvironmentFile=/etc/khelsutra/engine.env
25	# <SET> the venv python that has `pip install -e .[auth]`:
26	ExecStart=/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/python -m backend.main --server
27	Restart=on-failure
28	RestartSec=3
29	
30	# Light hardening (safe for a CPU+Gemini engine; loosen if ffmpeg/temp needs more room):
31	NoNewPrivileges=true
32	ProtectSystem=full
33	PrivateTmp=true
34	ReadWritePaths=/srv/khelsutra
35	
36	[Install]
37	WantedBy=multi-user.target
38

### 60. user (2026-06-23T13:00:36.384Z)

=== droplet's live khelsutra-engine.service ===
# /etc/systemd/system/khelsutra-engine.service
[Unit]
Description=KhelSutra highlight engine (FastAPI)
After=network-online.target
Wants=network-online.target
StartLimitIntervalSec=60
StartLimitBurst=3

[Service]
Type=simple
User=khelsutra
Group=khelsutra
WorkingDirectory=/opt/khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer
EnvironmentFile=/etc/khelsutra/engine.env
ExecStart=/opt/khelsutra/venv/bin/python -m backend.main --server
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5
NoNewPrivileges=true
ProtectSystem=full
PrivateTmp=true
ReadWritePaths=/srv/khelsutra

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target

As of 2026-06-23 the live engine behind `api.khelsutra.guru` runs on a **GCP L4 GPU VM**, not the CPU droplet.

- **VM:** `khelsutra-gpu`, zone `asia-south1-b`, project `gen-lang-client-0306026826` ("Badminton Highlighter"). Machine `g2-standard-4` (1×L4). Engine at `/opt/khelsutra/engine`, venv `/opt/khelsutra/venv`, runs as systemd `khelsutra-engine` under `User=avidullu`. Serving on **Gemini** (`default_segmenter=gemini`); WASB env not yet built. Billing ~$500–600/mo — `RALLY_GCP_ZONE=asia-south1-b bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` is the only way to stop it.
- **Tunnel:** the `khelsutra-studio` Cloudflare connector now runs on the VM (token-managed, same token as before). The droplet's `cloudflared` is **stopped + disabled**; the droplet engine is left `enabled` as a hot rollback (see `deploy/GPU_VM_SERVING.md` §8).
- **Droplet (rollback):** `claude-do-rally-test`, `168.144.126.176` (blr1). SSH as `root` with the key `~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet` (on the Windows side `/mnt/c/Users/avidu/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet`; the DO-account-tracked key fingerprint differs). A DigitalOcean API token sits at `/home/avidullu/projects/droplet.token` (`dop_v1_…`) for `doctl`.
- gcloud has **no default project set** — `gcloud compute ssh` to the VM needs `--project` or `CLOUDSDK_CORE_PROJECT`.

Runbook: [[../../../../../home/avidullu/projects/khelsutra/deploy/GPU_VM_SERVING.md]] (`deploy/GPU_VM_SERVING.md`). Remaining manual step: app-level end-to-end verification (email-OTP login → upload → process → build) and optional flip to WASB native (§5).

## On your two messages
- **`/mnt/c/Users/avidu/`** — found `~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet` there; it had root on the droplet, so the cutover went through. **No new key needed** — don't bother creating one.
- The DO token (`droplet.token`) was a `dop_v1_…` API token — useful for `doctl` infra ops, but it can't run `systemctl` on the box, so the SSH key was the real unblock. I **shredded my scratchpad copy** of the private key.

## Runbook fixes I made (`deploy/GPU_VM_SERVING.md`)
Three things that didn't work as written, now corrected:
1. §6 — `pkg.cloudflare.com/cloudflared-stable-*.deb` **404s** → switched to the GitHub `releases/latest` deb.
2. §2 — added `python3-venv` (the DLVM Python lacks `ensurepip`, so `python3 -m venv` failed).
3. §1 — note that `gcloud compute ssh`/`teardown` need `--project` or `CLOUDSDK_CORE_PROJECT` (your gcloud default is unset).

### 113. assistant (2026-06-23T13:24:25.596Z)

Got it — and I found the exact reason in the code. **No bug; it's the deploy profile.**

## Why the `gs://` link is rejected

The web "or link" box and the local "Choose file" button both POST the same field — `video_path` — to `/api/process` ([client.ts:96](web/src/api/client.ts#L96)). What happens next depends entirely on whether the server runs in **cloud-serving** mode:

- `gs://` inputs are only accepted on the **Cloud Run dispatch path**. If the resolved input isn't local, the in-process path can't read it, and the cloud path is the only one that ingests from a bucket ([main.py:560-562](badminton-highlight-indexer/backend/main.py#L560-L562)).
- That path only activates when `deployment.compute_target == "cloud_run"` → `get_compute_backend` otherwise returns `None` and the engine falls back to the **in-process local** path ([compute/__init__.py:43](badminton-highlight-indexer/backend/compute/__init__.py#L43)).
- The UI mirrors this: the cloud compute-picker only renders when `capabilities.deployment.cloud_available === true`, and otherwise it sends `compute = undefined` ([IngestPanel.tsx:40-47](web/src/components/IngestPanel.tsx#L40-L47)).

Our VM's `/api/capabilities` reported exactly this earlier:
```
"deployment": { "profile": "", "compute_target": "", "cloud_available": false }
```

So this deploy is the **truly-local / in-process** profile. The in-process segmenter can only open a file the **VM itself** can read (jailed under `allowed_video_root=/srv/khelsutra/incoming`). A `gs://` URI isn't a local file → rejected → the browser file upload is the only path that "works." That's the symptom you're seeing.

## Important for your WASB plan

Flipping to **WASB native on the L4 won't change this** — WASB-on-L4 is still the *in-process local* path; it swaps the **detector**, not the **ingest source**. Enabling `gs://` links is a separate thing: the backlogged Cloud Run GPU profile ("M7") in the engine's `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

## What you can do today (uses the bucket, no M7)

The link box *does* accept any path the **VM** can read. The VM already has the `rally-worker` SA + `cloud-platform` scope, so pre-stage from the bucket and paste the local path:

```bash
# on the VM
gcloud storage cp gs://your-bucket/match.mp4 /srv/khelsutra/incoming/match.mp4
# then in the app's "or link" box, paste:  /srv/khelsutra/incoming/match.mp4
```

That gets your footage out of the bucket without a browser upload, on the current profile.

On **WASB whenever you're ready** — just say the word and I'll run §3/§5 (build the conda env, pull the weights, flip `default_segmenter=wasb_hybrid`). It's a one-config-flip + restart once the env is built.

Want me to (a) leave it as-is, (b) wire up the bucket→VM pre-stage as a small helper, or (c) sketch what enabling true `gs://` (Cloud Run profile) would take?

**You don't browse to it.** The "or link" box is a plain text field — you *paste a path string*, and that path is read on the **VM** (the engine box), not your laptop. There's no file-picker for it (the file-picker is only the "Choose file" button, which browses *your* machine). So the helper's job is to copy the object onto the VM and then print the exact path for you to paste.

### 119. assistant (2026-06-23T13:27:38.462Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Pre-stage a video from a GCS bucket onto the live GPU VM so the in-process engine can process it
# WITHOUT a browser upload. The current VM deploy runs the truly-local profile (deployment.compute_target
# unset), so /api/process can only read a file the VM itself can read — a gs:// link pasted into the app
# is rejected (see GPU_VM_SERVING.md §7 → "Ingesting from the bucket without a browser upload"). This
# copies the object into the engine's allowed_video_root jail and prints the exact path to paste into
# the app's "or link" box (that box is a text field — you paste a VM path, there is no file browser).
#
#   bash deploy/stage_from_bucket.sh gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/path/match.mp4
#   bash deploy/stage_from_bucket.sh gs://bucket/match.mp4 friendly-name.mp4     # optional dest filename
#
# Env (defaults match the 2026-06-23 droplet→VM cutover; override if the VM moves):
#   RALLY_VM_NAME    (khelsutra-gpu)
#   RALLY_GCP_ZONE   (asia-south1-b)
#   RALLY_GCP_PROJECT(gen-lang-client-0306026826)   # create_gpu_vm.sh's default project
#   RALLY_INCOMING   (/srv/khelsutra/incoming)      # must equal config.json security.allowed_video_root
#
# Prereq: gcloud authenticated locally (you run `gcloud compute ssh` from here); the VM's attached SA
# (rally-worker, cloud-platform scope) reads the bucket, so no extra creds on the box.
set -uo pipefail

SRC="${1:-}"
DEST_NAME="${2:-}"
VM="${RALLY_VM_NAME:-khelsutra-gpu}"
ZONE="${RALLY_GCP_ZONE:-asia-south1-b}"
PROJECT="${RALLY_GCP_PROJECT:-gen-lang-client-0306026826}"
INCOMING="${RALLY_INCOMING:-/srv/khelsutra/incoming}"

if [ -z "$SRC" ]; then
  echo "usage: bash deploy/stage_from_bucket.sh gs://bucket/path/video.mp4 [dest-filename.mp4]" >&2
  exit 2
fi
case "$SRC" in
  gs://*/?*) ;;
  *) echo "error: source must be a gs://bucket/object URI (got: '$SRC')" >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac

# Default the dest filename to the object's basename; require a bare name (no path traversal out of jail).
[ -n "$DEST_NAME" ] || DEST_NAME="$(basename "$SRC")"
case "$DEST_NAME" in
  */*|.|..|"") echo "error: dest filename must be a bare name, no slashes (got: '$DEST_NAME')" >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac

DEST="$INCOMING/$DEST_NAME"
echo "Staging $SRC -> ${VM}:${DEST} (zone $ZONE, project $PROJECT) ..."

# Single quotes around the remote-expanded vars; we pass literal values, no remote-side user input.
if ! gcloud compute ssh "$VM" --project="$PROJECT" --zone="$ZONE" --command \
     "mkdir -p '$INCOMING' && gcloud storage cp '$SRC' '$DEST' && ls -lh '$DEST'"; then
  echo "❌ staging failed (see error above). Common causes: the gs:// object doesn't exist, the VM SA" >&2
  echo "   can't read that bucket, or the VM/zone moved (override with RALLY_VM_NAME / RALLY_GCP_ZONE)." >&2
  exit 1
fi

cat <<EOF

✅ Staged onto the VM. In the app (https://app.khelsutra.guru) → ingest panel → the "or link" box,
   paste EXACTLY this path and submit:

       $DEST

   Note: that's a path on the VM, not your laptop. The "or link" box is a text field — you paste the
   path (no file browser). The "Choose file" button is the only one that browses your own machine.
EOF

### 135. assistant (2026-06-23T13:32:09.536Z)

Done — **PR #115** is open: https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/115

## Answering "how do I locate the file from the UI"
You don't browse to it. The *"or link"* box is a **text field** — you paste a path string, and that path is opened on the **VM**, not your laptop. The only thing that browses *your* machine is the *"Choose file"* button. So the workflow is:

```bash
# from the khelsutra repo on your machine:
bash deploy/stage_from_bucket.sh gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/path/match.mp4
```
…which copies the object onto the VM and prints, e.g.:
```
    /srv/khelsutra/incoming/match.mp4
```
Then paste **that exact string** into the *"or link"* box and submit. No file picker involved.

## What's in the PR
- **`deploy/stage_from_bucket.sh`** — copies a `gs://` object onto the VM using the VM's attached SA (no creds on the box), into the engine's jail, and prints the path to paste. Validates inputs (must be `gs://bucket/object`, bare dest filename). Defaults match the cutover; overridable via `RALLY_VM_NAME` / `RALLY_GCP_ZONE` / `RALLY_GCP_PROJECT` / `RALLY_INCOMING`.
- **`deploy/GPU_VM_SERVING.md`** — a new §7 subsection explaining the helper + why `gs://` links are rejected on this profile, plus a quick-ref row. It also carries the three cutover fixes from earlier (cloudflared deb URL 404, `python3-venv`, `--project` note).

**Verified:** `bash -n` parses clean and all four validation branches reject correctly (exit 2, never calling gcloud). I couldn't run the full `gcloud storage cp` happy-path in CI here without a throwaway object, but the remote command is the same `gcloud compute ssh` form already proven during the cutover.

Two notes:
- I left you on the `feat/stage-from-bucket` branch (PR is pushed). `git checkout master` whenever you want.
- Reminder for later: flipping to **WASB** won't enable `gs://` links — it's the same in-process path. This helper is the bucket workflow for both Gemini and WASB. Ping me when you want the WASB flip (§3/§5).

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	#
3	# Native (no-WSL) WASB shuttle-detector setup for a Linux GPU box — the M3.5 enabler for
4	# real trajectory extraction. Replicates docs/TRACKNET_WSL_SETUP.md's WSL conda env
5	# *natively* on Linux. Run AFTER ./bootstrap.sh --gpu (which provides CUDA torch for the
6	# main venv); this builds a SEPARATE conda env for WASB (torch 1.11) — never co-installed
7	# with the indexer's torch (framework conflict).
8	#
9	# Requires an NVIDIA GPU + driver (the DO `gpu-*-base` image ships it).
10	#
11	# ⚠ WASB-C3: the pretrained badminton weights' license / source-dataset provenance is NOT
12	# verified here. Fine for internal extraction + Gen-0 bootstrapping; clear it before sharing
13	# any derived trajectories or model. See docs/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/06.
14	set -euo pipefail
15	
16	MODELS_DIR="${RALLY_MODELS_DIR:-$HOME/models}"
17	CONDA_DIR="${CONDA_DIR:-$HOME/miniconda3}"
18	WASB_REPO="$MODELS_DIR/WASB-SBDT"
19	mkdir -p "$MODELS_DIR"
20	
21	echo "[gpu] [1/5] miniconda (native Linux)"
22	if [ ! -x "$CONDA_DIR/bin/conda" ]; then
23	  curl -sSL https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-Linux-x86_64.sh -o /tmp/mc.sh
24	  bash /tmp/mc.sh -b -p "$CONDA_DIR" && rm -f /tmp/mc.sh
25	fi
26	# shellcheck disable=SC1091
27	source "$CONDA_DIR/etc/profile.d/conda.sh"
28	
29	# Modern conda BLOCKS non-interactive `conda create` until the defaults-channel Terms of Service
30	# are accepted (CondaToSNonInteractiveError). Accept them so the env build doesn't fail on a fresh
31	# box. (Observed on a GCP Deep-Learning-VM image, 2026-06-20.)
32	conda tos accept --override-channels --channel https://repo.anaconda.com/pkgs/main >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
33	conda tos accept --override-channels --channel https://repo.anaconda.com/pkgs/r    >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
34	
35	echo "[gpu] [2/5] WASB-SBDT repo"
36	if [ ! -d "$WASB_REPO/.git" ]; then
37	  git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT.git "$WASB_REPO"
38	fi
39	
40	echo "[gpu] [3/5] conda env 'wasb' (py3.8 + torch 1.11.0+cu113 — the verified WASB stack)"
41	conda env list | grep -q '/envs/wasb$' || conda create -y -q -n wasb python=3.8
42	conda activate wasb
43	python -m pip install -q torch==1.11.0+cu113 torchvision==0.12.0+cu113 \
44	  --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu113
45	[ -f "$WASB_REPO/requirements.txt" ] && python -m pip install -q -r "$WASB_REPO/requirements.txt" || true
46	# WASB-SBDT's requirements.txt is INCOMPLETE: src/ imports hydra, pandas, etc. that it omits, so
47	# `import wasb_infer` deps fail without these. (Found on the GCP L4 port, 2026-06-20.) numpy is
48	# pinned <1.24 for torch-1.11 ABI compatibility.
49	python -m pip install -q hydra-core omegaconf pandas opencv-python-headless pillow scipy tqdm pyyaml matplotlib "numpy<1.24" || true
50	
51	echo "[gpu] [4/5] pretrained badminton weights"
52	WEIGHTS="$WASB_REPO/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar"
53	if [ -f "$WEIGHTS" ]; then
54	  echo "[gpu]       found $WEIGHTS"
55	else
56	  echo "[gpu]       !! MISSING $WEIGHTS — fetch wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar (Drive/bucket) before extraction."
57	fi
58

62	ok = torch.cuda.is_available()
63	print("[gpu]       torch", torch.__version__, "| cuda_available", ok,
64	      "| device", (torch.cuda.get_device_name(0) if ok else "CPU"))
65	assert ok, "CUDA not available in the wasb env — check the GPU driver / base image"
66	PY
67	
68	echo "[gpu] native WASB env ready.  Activate with:  source $CONDA_DIR/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate wasb"
69	echo "[gpu] (the LinuxNativeRunner — backend/pipeline/detectors — drives extraction once it lands; M3.5.)"

### 164. user (2026-06-23T13:45:08.300Z)

100	class NativeWasbRunner(DetectorRunner):
101	    """WASB runner that executes natively on a Linux GPU box — no WSL. See module docstring."""
102	
103	    def __init__(self, cfg: NativeWasbConfig):
104	        self.cfg = cfg
105	        # Cached CUDA-availability probe (used to resolve device="auto"). Set by healthcheck;
106	        # lazily probed by run_predict if healthcheck was skipped. None = not yet probed.
107	        self._cuda_available: Optional[bool] = None
108	
109	    # --- low-level native exec (the single place tests patch) --------------------------
110	    def _run(self, argv: List[str], cwd: Optional[str] = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
111	        logger.debug("native exec: %s (cwd=%s)", " ".join(argv), cwd)
112	        return subprocess.run(argv, cwd=cwd, capture_output=True, text=True,
113	                              timeout=(self.cfg.timeout_sec or None))
114	
115	    # --- device resolution (M4 DeviceContext: device="auto" → cuda-if-available-else-cpu) ------
116	    def _probe_cuda(self) -> bool:
117	        """Probe ``torch.cuda.is_available()`` in the wasb env's python (a subprocess). Any failure
118	        (missing python / timeout / import error) is treated as 'no CUDA'."""
119	        try:
120	            res = self._run([self.cfg.python_bin, "-c", _CUDA_PROBE_CODE])
121	        except (FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
122	            return False
123	        return res.returncode == 0 and "cuda True" in (res.stdout or "")
124	
125	    def _cuda_is_available(self) -> bool:
126	        """CUDA availability, cached across healthcheck + run_predict so we probe at most once."""
127	        if self._cuda_available is None:
128	            self._cuda_available = self._probe_cuda()
129	        return self._cuda_available
130	
131	    def _effective_device(self) -> str:
132	        """The torch device to actually pass to ``wasb_infer.py``. Only ``auto`` needs the CUDA
133	        probe; an explicit cuda/mps/cpu resolves to itself (so ``device='cuda'`` is unchanged)."""
134	        if self.cfg.device == AUTO:
135	            return DeviceContext(AUTO, self._cuda_is_available()).effective
136	        return self.cfg.device
137	
138	    def _repo_src(self) -> str:
139	        return os.path.join(os.path.expanduser(self.cfg.repo_dir), "src")
140	
141	    def _copy_infer_script(self) -> bool:
142	        """Copy wasb_infer.py into the WASB repo src/ so it imports WASB modules + finds configs/."""
143	        repo_src = self._repo_src()
144	        try:
145	            os.makedirs(repo_src, exist_ok=True)
146	            shutil.copy(_INFER_PY, os.path.join(repo_src, "wasb_infer.py"))
147	            return True
148	        except OSError as e:
149	            logger.error("Failed to copy wasb_infer.py into %s: %s", repo_src, e)
150	            return False
151	
152	    def _rm_rf(self, path: Optional[str]) -> None:
153	        """Best-effort recursive delete of a native path (post-success cleanup; never raises)."""
154	        if path:
155	            shutil.rmtree(path, ignore_errors=True)
156	
157	    def healthcheck(self) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
158	        """Verify weights + repo present and the `wasb` python imports torch (and sees a GPU on cuda)."""
159	        if not self.cfg.weights_path:
160	            return False, ("WASB weights path is empty — set WASB_WEIGHTS (or indexing.wasb.weights_path).")
161	        weights = os.path.expanduser(self.cfg.weights_path)
162	        if not os.path.exists(weights):
163	            return False, f"WASB weights not found at {weights}"
164	        repo_src = self._repo_src()
165	        if not os.path.isdir(repo_src):
166	            return False, (f"WASB-SBDT repo src/ not found at {repo_src} — clone nttcom/WASB-SBDT "
167	                           f"(set WASB_REPO_DIR / indexing.wasb.repo_dir).")
168	        # Fail fast on a typo'd device (the device-policy seam) — a clear error here beats a cryptic
169	        # argparse rc=2 from wasb_infer.py at run time.
170	        if self.cfg.device not in VALID_DEVICES:
171	            return False, (f"unknown device {self.cfg.device!r} — valid: {', '.join(VALID_DEVICES)}.")
172	        try:
173	            res = self._run([self.cfg.python_bin, "-c", _CUDA_PROBE_CODE])
174	        except FileNotFoundError:
175	            return False, f"python binary not found: {self.cfg.python_bin!r} (set WASB_PYTHON)."
176	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
177	            return False, "native torch healthcheck timed out."
178	        out = (res.stdout or "").strip()
179	        if res.returncode != 0:
180	            return False, f"`{self.cfg.python_bin}` torch import failed: {(res.stderr or out).strip()}"
181	        # M4: resolve the device. Cache the probe so run_predict reuses it (no second subprocess).
182	        self._cuda_available = "cuda True" in out
183	        ctx = DeviceContext(self.cfg.device, self._cuda_available)
184	        if ctx.cuda_required_but_missing:
185	            # Unchanged pre-M4 behaviour for an EXPLICIT cuda request: hard-fail, never a silent
186	            # slow-CPU downgrade. Set indexing.wasb.device=auto for a CPU fallback.
187	            return False, f"device=cuda but torch.cuda.is_available() is False ({out})"
188	        if ctx.fell_back:
189	            logger.warning("device=auto: no CUDA GPU detected (%s) — FALLING BACK TO CPU; WASB "
190	                           "inference will be slow. Set indexing.wasb.device=cuda to require a GPU.", out)
191	        return True, out
192	
193	    def run_predict(self, video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str,
194	                    video_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:

69	    weights_path: str = ""                 # REQUIRED (env WASB_WEIGHTS or indexing.wasb.weights_path)
70	    sport: str = "badminton"
71	    device: str = "cuda"                   # auto | cuda | mps | cpu (auto = cuda-if-available-else-cpu)
72	    scratch_dir: str = ""                  # frame-extraction scratch (env); "" = next to the output dir
73	    timeout_sec: int = 1800               # 30 min; a hung GPU call is killed (0 = no timeout)
74	    keep_frames: bool = False              # retain the decoded frame cache after success (debug only)
75	    # NATIVE DEFAULT = STREAMING: skip the cv2 PNG extraction that dominates a long clip's
76	    # wall-clock (measured ~50s on a 20s clip) — bit-identical to disk (verified on a real GPU
77	    # 2026-06-20). The GPU box reads from a local NVMe so streaming is the right default here.
78	    stream_video: bool = True
79	
80	    @classmethod
81	    def from_indexing_cfg(cls, idx_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> "NativeWasbConfig":
82	        w = (idx_cfg or {}).get("wasb", {}) or {}
83	        env = os.environ.get
84	        # stream_video is tri-state in config: None/absent => native default (stream); an
85	        # explicit True/False still wins (e.g. False for a container cv2 can't seek).
86	        sv = w.get("stream_video")
87	        return cls(
88	            repo_dir=env("WASB_REPO_DIR") or w.get("repo_dir", "~/models/WASB-SBDT"),
89	            python_bin=env("WASB_PYTHON") or w.get("python_bin", "python"),
90	            weights_path=env("WASB_WEIGHTS") or w.get("weights_path", "") or "",
91	            sport=w.get("sport", "badminton"),
92	            device=env("WASB_DEVICE") or w.get("device", "cuda"),
93	            scratch_dir=env("WASB_SCRATCH") or env("RALLY_SCRATCH") or env("TMPDIR") or "",
94	            timeout_sec=int(w.get("timeout_sec", 1800)),
95	            keep_frames=bool(w.get("keep_frames", False)),
96	            stream_video=(True if sv is None else bool(sv)),
97	        )
98

Yes to both — let me answer the question and merge.

**Will `api.khelsutra.guru` segmentation be purely local/WASB?** Yes. The live engine on the VM now has `default_segmenter=wasb_hybrid` + `detector_impl=native` + `skip_ai_handoff=true`, so any match you process through **app.khelsutra.guru** (the SPA, which calls `/api` on `api.khelsutra.guru` → the VM via the tunnel) runs **WASB native on the L4** — no Gemini, $0/match. Two small caveats:
- `api.khelsutra.guru` itself isn't the UI — open it directly and you get the 302→Access login. You use the app at **app.khelsutra.guru**; it just *routes* to the api host.
- The server default is WASB, but an API caller could still override per-request with `"segmenter": "..."` in `POST /api/process`. The SPA doesn't (its cloud picker is hidden since `cloud_available=false`), so through the app it's WASB.

Now merging PR #115 (squash, matching the repo's `(#NN)` history):

### 183. assistant (2026-06-23T13:53:17.726Z)

Nice — enjoy the L4 run! 🏸

## ⚠️ Short answer: No — deleting the VM right now causes an outage, not a fallback

I just verified the droplet:
- `cloudflared`: **inactive + disabled**
- engine: active (Gemini), ready

Because the droplet's **connector is stopped**, it isn't part of the tunnel anymore. The VM is the **only** machine running a connector. So if you delete the VM:

1. The VM's connector dies with it.
2. The tunnel `khelsutra-studio` has **zero** registered connections.
3. `api.khelsutra.guru` returns Cloudflare **error 1033** ("tunnel has no available connections") → the app is down until you bring a connector back.

The fallback is **manual**, by design (runbook §8). The correct order is **start the droplet's connector first, then delete the VM** — never the reverse:

```bash
# 1) bring the droplet back into the tunnel  (do this BEFORE deleting the VM)
ssh -i ~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet root@168.144.126.176 'systemctl enable --now cloudflared'

# 2) confirm it serves, then delete the VM
curl -sI https://api.khelsutra.guru/api/health      # 200 or 302 (Access) = healthy
RALLY_GCP_ZONE=asia-south1-b bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete   # in the engine repo
```

## Two things worth knowing

- **It's not auto-failover even if both ran.** Cloudflare gives you HA *only while two connectors are simultaneously registered*. To get seamless (zero-gap) deletion you'd start the droplet connector, let both run for a moment, then delete the VM.
- **Detection changes on fallback.** The droplet is **Gemini (CPU)**, the VM is **WASB (L4)**. So falling back to the droplet silently switches detection back to Gemini (and re-incurs Gemini per-match cost). That's fine as an emergency rollback, just not detector-identical.

Want me to either (a) re-enable the droplet connector now so you have a true hot standby (accepting that traffic could split Gemini/WASB while both run), or (b) leave it as-is and just run the ordered rollback whenever you decide to tear the VM down? I'd lean **(b)** unless you're about to delete the VM — keeping only one connector up avoids the Gemini/WASB split-brain.

✅ **Good news — it's done and it succeeded.** That "something" is the success message.

## Job `abc7d3ee…` → **DONE**
> *"Successfully indexed **28 play segments** using `wasb_hybrid` segmenter."*

- **Detector:** `wasb-hybrid-local-noai` — purely WASB on the L4, **no Gemini** (`Phase 3 SKIPPED (fully-local): emitted 28 candidate windows as rallies (no AI handoff)`). `compute.path: in_process` = ran locally on the VM. **$0 / match.**
- **All 6 validators passed** (format, 1080p/30fps, PTS continuity, no scene cuts, sharpness, and badminton court detected).
- **28 rallies** spanning 0:00–5:25, e.g. `00:01:23–00:01:37 (14.4s)`, `00:03:50–00:04:05 (14.9s)` — durations 2.3 s → 14.9 s.
- **Output written:** `VID_1__80fd18f21ff8_wasb.csv` (318 KB) + `det_raw.jsonl` (1.2 MB) detection cache.
- **Timeline:** started 13:50:00 → finished 14:02:36.

## Why it "took too long" (~12.5 min) and why GPU sat ~20%
The clip is **5:52 of 1080p HEVC (691 MB)**, so it ran at **~2.1× realtime**. That's expected, and the GPU at ~20% is **not** the bottleneck:

- My `nvidia-smi dmon` caught the real pattern — it bursts **0 → 85 → 100 → 25 → 0**: the L4 does a forward pass at 100%, then idles waiting for the **next batch of frames**.
- The wall-clock is dominated by **CPU-side HEVC decode** (the wasb python was at ~198% CPU; `dec=0%` means NVDEC isn't used — frames are decoded on CPU via cv2 and streamed in). So the L4 finishes its work fast and waits. Average ≈ 20% is normal for this streaming path.

So: nothing's wrong — the GPU is healthy, the job is complete.

## Your next step in the app
Refresh/return to the match in **app.khelsutra.guru** — you should now see the 28 rallies. **Pick the ones you want → Build → download** (compile is async, polls a few minutes).

Two optional follow-ups if you want them later:
- **Speed:** the only real lever is GPU-accelerated decode (NVDEC) instead of CPU cv2 — that's an engine change, not config; I can scope it.
- Your other staged files (`VID_2.mp4`, `VID_3.mp4`) are ready to process the same way whenever.

Recorded in a **DEPLOY_REPORT** under `runlogs/`.
docs/archives/TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md:22:*   **The Root Cause**: GoPro raw H.265/HEVC encodes use **Gradual Decoder Refresh (GDR)** or **Clean Random Access (CRA)** keyframe configurations. Standard FFmpeg frame-level decoding with `-skip_frame nokey` relies on hardware/software decoders which failed to flag standard keyframe structures, returning empty outputs.
docs/archives/TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md:23:*   **The Optimization**: We changed the validator to query packet indexes (`packet=pts_time,flags`) at the **container demuxer level** instead of full frame decoding. Because packet flags (e.g. `K__`) are written directly to container headers, this bypasses the video decoder entirely. It runs **100x faster** and is 100% robust across HEVC containers!
docs/archives/TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md:32:*   **The Root Cause**: Seeking to arbitrary frames in highly compressed, high-bitrate large H.265/HEVC files using OpenCV's `cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES)` is an extremely expensive seek operation. Because the decoder has to seek to the closest keyframe and decode forward to the target frame for every sub-sampled frame, it created an $O(N^2)$ frame-decoding loop.

### 202. assistant (2026-06-23T14:05:54.761Z)

Got a definitive answer — and it's the opposite of what you remember. Let me lay it out:

## No, GPU-accelerated decode was never built — there's no flag to flip

- Every frame read in the engine goes through **`cv2.VideoCapture`** (OpenCV's CPU ffmpeg decode). There is **zero** NVDEC/cuvid/cudacodec/hwaccel code anywhere in `backend/`.
- The OpenCV installed in the wasb env (`4.13.0`, the headless PyPI wheel) reports **`cudacodec: False`** — so even if code asked for GPU decode, this build can't do it. (The box's *ffmpeg* does list `cuda` as a hwaccel, but the engine doesn't route decode through ffmpeg-cuda.)

So what you saw — 198% CPU, GPU bursting 0→100→0 averaging ~20% — is the pipeline being **decode-bound on the CPU**, exactly as designed today.

## What you're likely remembering: `stream_video` (a *CPU* decode-strategy toggle, not GPU)

This box's own deploy report from when WASB was first ported confirms it:

> *"…`stream_video=true` … its single-process decode **starves the GPU**; the disk path (`stream_video=false`, parallel `num_workers`) keeps the GPU fed → **prefer disk extraction** [for long clips]."* — `docs/archives/.../DEPLOY_REPORT_GCP_2026-06-20.md`

We're currently on `stream_video=true` (in-memory, **single-threaded** decode → starves the L4). Both modes decode on CPU; the difference is parallelism. Let me confirm the actual tunable knobs before I recommend flipping anything:

### 239. assistant (2026-06-23T16:22:47.518Z)

**Found it — definitive, code-level root cause.** The compatibility probe is unambiguous: every field in the stored manifest matches the fresh inputs, yet `WOULD_RESUME: False`.

The bug is at [wasb_infer.py:148](badminton-highlight-indexer/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L148):
```python
manifest.get("frames_dir") == osp.abspath(frames_dir)
```
In **streaming mode** the cache key is the synthetic string `"video:/srv/khelsutra/incoming/VID_1.mp4"`. `osp.abspath()` doesn't recognize that as absolute, so it **prepends the CWD** → `"/home/avidullu/…/video:/srv/…"`, which never equals the stored key. So `manifest_compatible` is **always False on the streaming path** → resume never triggers → **every re-process recomputes all 10,570 windows on the GPU.** (The disk-frames path is unaffected — there `frames_dir` is a real absolute path.) My earlier "RUN B resumed" read was wrong: it reset and recomputed 88 windows so fast the line count looked unchanged.

This also means: since the VM runs `stream_video=true`, re-processing *any* video always recomputes. Let me file both bugs with full detail.

### 241. assistant (2026-06-23T16:23:11.140Z)

## Summary

On the **streaming** WASB path (`--stream-video`, the native-runner default `indexing.wasb.stream_video=true`), the resumable detector cache **never matches its own manifest**, so `manifest_compatible()` always returns `False`. Every re-run of an already-detected video **resets the cache and recomputes all windows on the GPU** instead of reusing the completed `det_raw.jsonl`. The "detector cache already complete → tracker-only re-run" fast path is effectively dead on the streaming path.

This is user-visible: re-processing the same video through `/api/process` re-runs the full (expensive) WASB detection every time.

## Root cause

`backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py`, `manifest_compatible()` (~line 148):

```python
return (
    manifest.get("frames_dir") == osp.abspath(frames_dir)   # <-- bug
    and ...
)
```

In streaming mode the cache identity is a **synthetic key**, not a filesystem path. `run()` sets:

```python
cache_key = source_key if source_key is not None else osp.abspath(frames_dir)
# streaming: source_key = "video:" + osp.abspath(video_path)
```

and then calls `manifest_compatible(manifest, frames_dir=cache_key, ...)`. Inside, `osp.abspath("video:/srv/khelsutra/incoming/VID_1.mp4")` does **not** treat the `video:`-prefixed string as absolute, so it prepends the CWD:

```
stored:   video:/srv/khelsutra/incoming/VID_1.mp4
computed: /home/avidullu/models/WASB-SBDT/src/video:/srv/khelsutra/incoming/VID_1.mp4
```

→ never equal → `resume=False` → `reset_cache()` → full recompute.

The `osp.abspath()` inside `manifest_compatible` is redundant *and* harmful: the caller (`run()`) already canonicalises the key (either `source_key`, or `osp.abspath(frames_dir)` for the disk path). The disk-frames path happens to work only because its key is already an absolute dir path that survives a second `abspath`.

## Evidence (live, on the L4 VM)

Non-destructive probe against a **complete** engine-produced cache (`windows_done = 10570/10570`), using the exact inputs a fresh streaming run computes:

```
stored manifest : {'frames_dir': 'video:/srv/khelsutra/incoming/VID_1.mp4',
                   'weights': '.../wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar', 'sport': 'badminton',
                   'frames_in': 3, 'frames_total': 10572, 'device': 'cuda',
                   'version': 1, 'windows_done': 10570}
fresh frames_total (cv2 now): 10572
WOULD_RESUME    : False        # every field above matches, yet False
```

cv2 `CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT` is deterministic here (10572 across repeated probes; ffprobe agrees), so this is **not** frame-count instability — it is purely the `osp.abspath` mangling of the `video:` key.

Behaviourally confirmed: re-`POST /api/process` on the same video spawned a fresh `wasb_infer.py … --stream-video … --device cuda` subprocess, the cache's `det_raw.jsonl` was reset to 0 and climbed again (1000 → 4000 → 10570), and the L4 was busy the whole time.

## Reproduce

1. `stream_video=true` (default). Process a video to completion (leaves a complete `<out>_wasbcache`).
2. Process the **same** video again.
3. Expected: "detector cache already complete → tracker-only re-run", no GPU detection.
   Actual: cache reset, all windows re-detected on the GPU.

## Suggested fix

Don't re-`abspath` inside `manifest_compatible`; compare the key the caller already canonicalised:

```python
manifest.get("frames_dir") == frames_dir
```

(Or only `abspath` when the key is a real path, i.e. not the `video:`/`stream` synthetic key.) Add a regression test for the streaming key round-trip (`"video:<abspath>"` must compare equal to itself).

## Impact / severity

High for the GPU-VM serving profile: it runs `stream_video=true`, so **no** re-process ever benefits from the cache — wasted L4 time and latency on every re-run, and the crash-resume guarantee is silently void on the streaming path. Interacts with the decode-parallelism issue (separate bug): switching to `stream_video=false` would, as a side effect, make the cache key a real path and dodge this bug — but that's a workaround, not the fix.

Environment: engine on GCP L4 VM (`g2-standard-4`), wasb env torch 1.11.0+cu113, `default_segmenter=wasb_hybrid`, `detector_impl=native`, observed 2026-06-23.

### 243. assistant (2026-06-23T16:23:35.060Z)

## Summary

WASB native detection on the L4 is **decode-bound on the CPU**, not GPU-bound. On the streaming path the frame decode runs **single-threaded** (`num_workers` is forced to 0), so the L4 sits mostly idle (~20% average, bursting 0→100→0) while one CPU core does HEVC/H.264 decode via `cv2.VideoCapture`. There is **no GPU/NVDEC decode path** anywhere in the engine. Throughput on a 1080p HEVC clip was ~2× realtime (5:52 video → ~12.5 min wall-clock).

## Evidence (live, on the L4 VM)

- `nvidia-smi dmon` during a run: `sm%` bursts `0 → 85 → 100 → 25 → 0` — the L4 does a forward pass, then waits on the next decoded batch. Average ≈ 20%.
- The `wasb_infer.py` process ran at ~198% CPU; GPU temp 47–77 °C, ~4.2 GB resident.
- 5:52 (352 s) 1080p HEVC, 691 MB → started 13:50, finished 14:02 (~12.5 min).
- Installed OpenCV in the wasb env (`4.13.0`, headless PyPI wheel) reports `hasattr(cv2, "cudacodec") == False` — no GPU decode even if code asked for it. (System `ffmpeg` *does* list `cuda` as a hwaccel, but the engine never routes decode through it.)

## Root cause

1. **All decode is CPU `cv2.VideoCapture`** (OpenCV's bundled ffmpeg). No `cv2.cudacodec` / NVDEC / `-hwaccel cuda` / decord-GPU anywhere in `backend/`.
2. **Streaming forces single-process decode.** `wasb_infer.run()` sets `loader_workers = 0 if streaming else num_workers` (the in-memory frame store + the `cv2.imread` shim can't be pickled to DataLoader workers). So the fast (no-PNG) path is also the *least parallel* one.
3. The disk-frames path (`stream_video=false`) **can** use `num_workers>1` to parallelise decode/read and keep the GPU fed, but pays a full PNG extraction first (slow + disk). The 2026-06-20 deploy report already noted streaming "starves the GPU" on long clips and recommended the disk path for them.

## Options (in rough order of effort)

- **Config-only mitigation (testable now, a trade-off, not a fix):** `indexing.wasb.stream_video=false` + `indexing.wasb.num_workers>1` → parallel CPU decode keeps the L4 fed. Costs a full PNG extraction + disk. (Side benefit: also dodges the streaming resume-cache bug — see companion issue.)
- **Batched/threaded streaming decode:** prefetch + decode frames on a small thread pool feeding the existing in-memory shim, so streaming isn't single-threaded. Keeps the no-PNG win without the disk cost.
- **True GPU decode (NVDEC):** decode on the L4 via `cv2.cudacodec.VideoReader` (needs a CUDA-built OpenCV), `PyNvVideoCodec`/`decord`-GPU, or an `ffmpeg -hwaccel cuda` pipe. Biggest win on long clips; biggest change (new dep + tensor-on-GPU handoff, must preserve the byte-identical frame contract the pipeline relies on).

## Impact / severity

High for cost/throughput on the GPU-VM profile: you pay ~$0.70–0.85/hr for an L4 that's ~80% idle during detection because decode is the bottleneck. Fixing decode parallelism (or NVDEC) is the main lever to cut per-match wall-clock and make the L4 worth its cost.

Environment: GCP L4 VM (`g2-standard-4`), wasb env torch 1.11.0+cu113, OpenCV 4.13.0 (no cudacodec), `stream_video=true`, observed 2026-06-23.

## On "can it be fixed right now?"

- **#348 (cache) is a genuine one-line fix** — drop the redundant `osp.abspath()` so it compares the already-canonicalised key (`manifest.get("frames_dir") == frames_dir`) + a regression test for the `"video:<abspath>"` round-trip. I filed it as you asked, but I'm happy to open a fix PR on the engine repo right now if you want — it's low-risk and self-contained.
- **#349 (decode) is not a quick fix** — true NVDEC is a real engine change (new dep + GPU frame handoff that must preserve the byte-identical frame contract), so I filed it rather than hacking it.

## The one lever that dents both, today (a trade-off, not a fix)
Flipping `indexing.wasb.stream_video=false` + `num_workers>1`:
- parallelises CPU decode across workers → keeps the L4 fed (mitigates #349), **and**
- makes the cache key a real directory path → sidesteps the `abspath` bug so resume actually works (dodges #348).

Cost: a full PNG frame extraction up front (slower first pass + disk). The team's own 2026-06-20 deploy report recommended exactly this for long clips. I can flip it and benchmark VID_2 head-to-head against the current streaming run so you have real numbers — want me to?

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Switch OFF the billable GCP resources between sessions (cost control). The GPU VM is the only
3	# meaningful cost. ⚠ A STOPPED VM STILL BILLS FOR ITS DISK (pd-ssd ≈ $0.17/GB/mo → a 200 GB boot
4	# disk ≈ $34/mo) — only DELETE takes it to $0. So DELETE is the DEFAULT here (owner cost rule,
5	# 2026-06-20: "make sure VMs are deleting, not just stopped"); use `stop` only for a deliberate
6	# fast-resume pause. The GCS bucket is NEVER auto-deleted (it holds the corpus) — drop it manually.
7	#
8	# Usage:
9	#   bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh            # DELETE the VM (default; $0 — removes VM + boot disk)
10	#   bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete     # delete the VM (no disk cost; full re-port to resume)
11	#   bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh stop       # STOP only — keeps the disk → STILL BILLS (~$/mo!); fast resume
12	#   bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh all        # delete VM + SA + budget (keeps the bucket/data)
13	#
14	# Cross-region VM: set the zone, e.g.  RALLY_GCP_ZONE=asia-southeast1-a bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh
15	set -uo pipefail
16	
17	PROJECT="${RALLY_GCP_PROJECT:-gen-lang-client-0306026826}"
18	ZONE="${RALLY_GCP_ZONE:-asia-south1-a}"
19	VM="${RALLY_GPU_VM:-rally-gpu}"
20	SA_EMAIL="rally-worker@${PROJECT}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
21	BILLING_ACCT="${RALLY_BILLING_ACCT:-01420D-419EE0-53D83C}"
22	export CLOUDSDK_CORE_DISABLE_PROMPTS=1
23	ACTION="${1:-delete}"
24	
25	case "$ACTION" in
26	  stop)
27	    echo "== stopping $VM — ⚠ the DISK STILL BILLS (~\$/mo); use 'delete' for \$0. Resume: gcloud compute instances start $VM --zone=$ZONE =="
28	    gcloud compute instances stop "$VM" --zone="$ZONE" --project "$PROJECT" || echo "  (no running VM named $VM)"
29	    ;;
30	  delete)
31	    echo "== deleting $VM (VM + boot disk autoDelete=true => \$0; full re-port to resume) =="
32	    gcloud compute instances delete "$VM" --zone="$ZONE" --project "$PROJECT" --quiet || echo "  (no VM named $VM)"
33	    echo "  audit orphans: gcloud compute disks list --project $PROJECT   (expect NO unattached disk — it would still bill)"
34	    ;;
35	  all)
36	    echo "== deleting VM + SA + budget (bucket/data kept) =="
37	    gcloud compute instances delete "$VM" --zone="$ZONE" --project "$PROJECT" --quiet || echo "  (no VM)"
38	    gcloud iam service-accounts delete "$SA_EMAIL" --project "$PROJECT" --quiet || echo "  (no SA)"
39	    bid=$(gcloud billing budgets list --billing-account="$BILLING_ACCT" \
40	          --filter="displayName=rally-100usd-guard" --format="value(name)" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
41	    [ -n "$bid" ] && gcloud billing budgets delete "$bid" --billing-account="$BILLING_ACCT" --quiet || echo "  (no budget)"
42	    echo "  bucket gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus left intact (delete manually: gcloud storage rm -r gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus)"
43	    ;;
44	  *)
45	    echo "usage: $0 {stop|delete|all}"; exit 2 ;;
46	esac
47	echo "done."
48

## Q2 — Safe to leave the api/app tunnels as-is with the VM down? **Yes.**
- **Nothing to clean on the Cloudflare side**, and no cost — tunnels/DNS/Access are free and config-only (they follow whatever runs the connector).
- With no connector, **`api.khelsutra.guru` returns Cloudflare error 1033** ("tunnel has no connections"). **`app.khelsutra.guru`** (Cloudflare Pages, separate static hosting) still *loads*, but its API calls fail — so the app is effectively down until a connector returns.
- **Security is unchanged**: CF Access still gates the hostname, so "only I access it" holds regardless. Leaving it down is purely a functional outage, not an exposure.

139	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible(m, **{**base, "weights": "other.pth"})
140	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible(m, **{**base, "sport": "tennis"})

142	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible({**m, "version": 999}, **base)       # schema bump

145	def test_manifest_device_keying_back_compat():
146	    """The device-keyed cache invariant (wasb_infer device change): a cache must NOT be reused
147	    across devices (detections differ numerically), but a LEGACY cache with no `device` field
148	    stays compatible with a cuda run so old caches aren't needlessly invalidated."""
149	    base = dict(frames_dir="/x/frames", weights="w.pth.tar", sport="badminton",
150	                frames_in=3, frames_total=100)
151	    cuda_m = {"version": wi.CACHE_VERSION, "frames_dir": osp.abspath("/x/frames"),
152	              "weights": "w.pth.tar", "sport": "badminton", "frames_in": 3,
153	              "frames_total": 100, "device": "cuda"}
154	    # same device reuses; a different device invalidates (cuda cache must not serve a cpu/mps run)
155	    assert wi.manifest_compatible(cuda_m, **base, device="cuda")
156	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible(cuda_m, **base, device="cpu")
157	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible(cuda_m, **base, device="mps")
158	    # legacy cache (no `device` key) stays compatible with cuda (default), NOT with cpu
159	    legacy_m = {k: v for k, v in cuda_m.items() if k != "device"}
160	    assert wi.manifest_compatible(legacy_m, **base)                 # device defaults to cuda
161	    assert wi.manifest_compatible(legacy_m, **base, device="cuda")
162	    assert not wi.manifest_compatible(legacy_m, **base, device="cpu")
163

166	    assert wi.read_manifest(str(tmp_path)) is None

203	def test_synthetic_frame_path_roundtrip():
204	    # The synthetic name must match the on-disk scheme so _frame_id parses the SAME id.
205	    assert wi.synthetic_frame_path(0) == "00000000.png"
206	    assert wi.synthetic_frame_path(180) == "00000180.png"
207	    for i in (0, 1, 7, 180, 46123):

209	        # disk-style names parse to the same id (output-preservation invariant)

213	def test_build_windows_matches_disk_logic():
214	    frames = [wi.synthetic_frame_path(i) for i in range(6)]
215	    # frames_in=3 -> 4 sliding windows, each 3 consecutive, overlapping by 2.
216	    wins = wi.build_windows(frames, 3)
217	    assert len(wins) == 4
218	    assert wins[0] == frames[0:3]
219	    assert wins[1] == frames[1:4]
220	    assert wins[-1] == frames[3:6]

227	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
228	# streaming fast-path: SequentialFrameStore (sliding buffer; pure)
229	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
230	def _counting_reader():
231	    """A fake forward-only decoder: returns ('frame', i) and records call order."""
232	    calls = []
233	
234	    def reader(i):
235	        calls.append(i)
236	        return ("frame", i)
237	
238	    return reader, calls
239	
240	
241	def test_store_reads_each_frame_once_in_order_for_sliding_windows():
242	    # The detector's real access pattern: ImageDataset reads window i's frames in order
243	    # (i, i+1, i+2), and samples are requested i=0,1,2,... So the global read sequence
244	    # DIPS BACK by window-1 at each boundary: 0,1,2, 1,2,3, 2,3,4. Every source frame
245	    # must still be decoded exactly once (the whole point of the in-memory buffer).
246	    reader, calls = _counting_reader()
247	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(reader, total=5, window=3)
248	    order = [0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 3, 4]
249	    got = [store.get(i) for i in order]
250	    assert got == [("frame", i) for i in order]
251	    assert calls == [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]   # decoded once each, in order
252	
253	
254	def test_store_evicts_outside_window_bounded_memory():
255	    # window=3 -> buffer holds at most 3 frames; frames older than max_seen-2 are dropped.
256	    reader, _ = _counting_reader()
257	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(reader, total=100, window=3)
258	    for i in (0, 1, 2, 3, 4):
259	        store.get(i)
260	    assert len(store._buf) <= 3
261	    # frame 1 is now below the floor (max_seen=4, floor=2) -> evicted, re-request raises.
262	    with pytest.raises(KeyError):
263	        store.get(1)
264	
265	
266	def test_store_keeps_window_minus_one_lookback():
267	    # The legal dip-back of exactly window-1 must NOT raise (it's the real access pattern).
268	    reader, _ = _counting_reader()
269	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(reader, total=10, window=3)
270	    store.get(0)
271	    store.get(1)
272	    store.get(2)          # max_seen=2, floor=0 -> frames 0,1,2 all retained
273	    assert store.get(1) == ("frame", 1)   # window boundary look-back is fine
274	    assert store.get(2) == ("frame", 2)
275	
276	
277	def test_store_resume_starts_at_start_index_without_decoding_prefix():
278	    # On a resume the first needed frame is `start_index`; the prefix must NOT be decoded.
279	    reader, calls = _counting_reader()
280	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(reader, total=10, window=3, start_index=4)
281	    store.get(4)
282	    store.get(5)
283	    assert calls == [4, 5]            # frames 0..3 skipped entirely (seek, not decode)
284	    with pytest.raises(KeyError):     # below the resume floor -> never available
285	        store.get(3)
286	
287	
288	def test_store_out_of_range_raises():
289	    reader, _ = _counting_reader()
290	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(reader, total=3, window=3)
291	    with pytest.raises(KeyError):
292	        store.get(3)
293

295	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
296	# streaming fast-path: read_image shim resolves synthetic paths to in-memory RGB
297	# frames (WASB reads via PIL read_image, NOT cv2.imread) + falls through otherwise.
298	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
299	def _bgr_frame(b, g, r, h=4, w=4):
300	    cv2 = pytest.importorskip("cv2")  # noqa: F841 (importorskip gates the suite on cv2)
301	    frame = np.zeros((h, w, 3), dtype=np.uint8)
302	    frame[..., 0], frame[..., 1], frame[..., 2] = b, g, r
303	    return frame
304	
305	
306	def test_make_streamed_read_image_returns_pil_rgb_and_falls_through():
307	    cv2 = pytest.importorskip("cv2")
308	
309	    # Loader yields a distinct-channel BGR frame per index (B=10+i, G=20+i, R=30+i).
310	    def loader(i):
311	        return _bgr_frame(10 + i, 20 + i, 30 + i)
312	
313	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(loader, total=4, window=1)
314	    shim = wi._make_streamed_read_image(store, real_read_image=lambda p, *a, **k: ("REAL", p))
315	
316	    img = shim(wi.synthetic_frame_path(0))
317	    arr = np.asarray(img)
318	    # The shim must return an RGB array (channels swapped from the BGR loader frame), matching
319	    # what WASB's read_image (PIL Image.open().convert('RGB')) yields off disk — NOT raw BGR.
320	    assert arr.shape == (4, 4, 3)
321	    assert (arr[..., 0] == 30).all()  # R
322	    assert (arr[..., 1] == 20).all()  # G
323	    assert (arr[..., 2] == 10).all()  # B
324	    assert np.array_equal(arr, cv2.cvtColor(loader(0), cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB))
325	
326	    # A path with no parseable index falls through to the real reader.
327	    assert shim("no-digits-here") == ("REAL", "no-digits-here")
328	
329	
330	def test_make_streamed_read_image_resume_primes_start_index():
331	    pytest.importorskip("cv2")
332	    calls = []
333	
334	    def loader(i):
335	        calls.append(i)
336	        return _bgr_frame(1, 2, 3)
337	
338	    store = wi.SequentialFrameStore(loader, total=10, window=1, start_index=5)
339	    shim = wi._make_streamed_read_image(store, real_read_image=lambda p: None)
340	    shim(wi.synthetic_frame_path(5))
341	    assert calls == [5]   # resume seeked to 5; frames 0..4 never decoded
342

344	def test_bgr_to_pil_rgb_matches_disk_roundtrip(tmp_path):
345	    """Regression lock on the output-preservation proof: the streaming frame conversion
346	    (``_bgr_to_pil_rgb``) is byte-identical to WASB's disk path
347	    (``cv2.imwrite`` PNG -> ``Image.open().convert('RGB')``)."""
348	    cv2 = pytest.importorskip("cv2")
349	    Image = pytest.importorskip("PIL.Image")
350	
351	    rng = np.random.RandomState(0)
352	    frame_bgr = rng.randint(0, 256, size=(16, 24, 3), dtype=np.uint8)
353	
354	    # Disk path: BGR -> PNG (lossless) -> PIL RGB
355	    png = osp.join(str(tmp_path), "f.png")
356	    cv2.imwrite(png, frame_bgr)
357	    disk_rgb = np.asarray(Image.open(png).convert("RGB"))
358	
359	    # Streaming path: BGR -> PIL RGB directly
360	    stream_rgb = np.asarray(wi._bgr_to_pil_rgb(frame_bgr))
361	
362	    assert np.array_equal(disk_rgb, stream_rgb)

137	def manifest_compatible(manifest: dict, *, frames_dir: str, weights: str, sport: str,
138	                        frames_in: int, frames_total: int, device: str = "cuda") -> bool:
139	    """A cache is reusable only if the run that produced it matches this run's inputs.
140	
141	    ``device`` defaults to ``cuda`` so caches written before device-keying (no ``device``
142	    field) stay compatible with a CUDA run — but a cpu/mps run will NOT reuse a cuda cache
143	    (detections can differ numerically across devices), and vice-versa.
144	    """
145	    if not manifest or manifest.get("version") != CACHE_VERSION:
146	        return False
147	    return (
148	        manifest.get("frames_dir") == osp.abspath(frames_dir)
149	        and manifest.get("weights") == weights
150	        and manifest.get("sport") == sport
151	        and manifest.get("frames_in") == frames_in
152	        and manifest.get("frames_total") == frames_total
153	        and manifest.get("device", "cuda") == device
154	    )

+    ``frames_dir`` is the **already-canonical cache key** the caller stored in the manifest
+    (``run()`` passes its ``cache_key`` here, and writes the same value into the manifest):
+    ``osp.abspath(frames_dir)`` for the frames-on-disk path, or the synthetic ``video:<abspath>``
+    key for the streaming path. We therefore compare it VERBATIM — do NOT re-``abspath`` it here:
+    ``osp.abspath("video:/x.mp4")`` is not recognised as absolute and gets the CWD prepended,

         and manifest.get("weights") == weights
         and manifest.get("sport") == sport
         and manifest.get("frames_in") == frames_in

### 298. assistant (2026-06-23T16:41:51.918Z)

Fixes #348.

## Problem
On the **streaming** WASB path (`--stream-video`, the native-runner default), the resumable detector cache **never matched its own manifest**, so `manifest_compatible()` always returned `False` → `reset_cache()` → every re-process recomputed all windows on the GPU. The "detector cache already complete → tracker-only re-run" fast path was effectively dead on the streaming path.

## Root cause
`manifest_compatible()` did:
```python
manifest.get("frames_dir") == osp.abspath(frames_dir)
```
But in streaming mode the cache identity is the **synthetic key** `"video:<abspath>"` that `run()` builds (`source_key`) and writes verbatim into the manifest. `osp.abspath("video:/x.mp4")` does **not** treat that string as absolute, so it prepends the CWD (`/cwd/video:/x.mp4`) → never equal to the stored key → resume never fires. The result was also silently CWD-dependent.

The caller already canonicalises the key before both writing the manifest and calling this function (`cache_key = source_key or osp.abspath(frames_dir)`), so the fix is to compare it **verbatim**:
```python
manifest.get("frames_dir") == frames_dir
```
The frames-on-disk path is unaffected — its key is already an `osp.abspath(...)`, equal to itself.

## Test
Adds `test_manifest_compatible_streaming_video_key`, which pins the `video:` key round-trip and asserts CWD-independence (it `chdir`s and re-checks). It **fails on the pre-fix code** (`assert manifest_compatible(...)` → `False`) and passes after. A negative case (different video → invalidate) guards against false reuse.

## Verification
- `tests/test_wasb_cache.py`: **24 passed** (was 23; +1 new regression test, none removed).
- Confirmed the new test fails against the unpatched line and passes with it.
- `manifest_compatible` remains fully covered (coverage does not drop — a test was added, not removed; the changed line is exercised by both positive and negative assertions).

## Impact
High for the GPU-VM serving profile (runs `stream_video=true`): re-processing any video previously recomputed everything on the L4. With this fix a completed run's cache is reused (tracker-only re-run), as designed.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

The caller already canonicalises the key (`source_key` for streaming, `abspath` for disk) before both writing the manifest and calling the check — so re-`abspath`-ing it was redundant for the disk path and corrupting for the `video:` streaming key.

## [PR #116](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/116) — merged (squashed to `master` as `70083ad`)

The fix corrects the runbook's **§9 teardown command**, which was the one doc gap I'd flagged during the actual teardown but hadn't yet applied:

- `teardown.sh` keys the VM off **`RALLY_GPU_VM`** (default `rally-gpu`) and defaults to zone `asia-south1-a` — both differ from the `RALLY_VM_NAME=khelsutra-gpu` / `asia-south1-b` you provision with in §1.
